17	---
18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

</details>

      "content": "---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",
      "numLines": 276,

    }

20	# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）
21	
22	> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。
23	> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）
28	
29	### 1.1 数字必须有源头
30	
31	- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件
32	- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据
33	- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报
34	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance
35	
36	> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。
37	
38	### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用
39	
40	- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol
41	- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明
42	- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较
43	- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 "at unmatched volumes"）
44	
45	> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。
46	
47	### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活
48	
49	- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文
50	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复
51	- 术语修正后不可回退（如 "same norm" → "same per-layer standard deviation"）
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配
56	
57	### 2.1 Claim 强度规则
58	
59	| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |
60	|----------|--------------|
61	| 单 seed, 单数据集 | "preliminary observation", "initial evidence" |
62	| 单 seed, 多数据集 | "suggestive pattern", "indicative trend" |
63	| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 "we find" |
64	| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 "robust", "consistent" |
65	| 统计检验显著 | 可用 "significant", 附 p-value |
66	| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 "scale-invariant" 等 |
67	
68	### 2.2 禁止的过强表述
69	
70	- "remarkably stable" → 改为 "relatively stable" 或给出量化波动范围
71	- "confirmed" → 仅 "suggestive" 级别数据不能用
72	- "scale-invariant" → single-seed 验证不够
73	- "We attribute X to Y" → 如果是假说，必须改为 "We hypothesize" + "requiring causal verification"
74	- "roughly twice" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似
75	- "domain-agnostic" → 改为 "robust across N domains tested"
76	
77	### 2.3 Claim 降级机制
78	
79	当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：
80	- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落
81	- 硬 claim → 加 caveat 的软 claim
82	- 正文数字 → 移入 Appendix
83	
84	---
85	
86	## 三、叙事与 Storytelling
87	
88	### 3.1 结构原则
89	
90	- 核心 paradox/发现之间必须有**过渡句**桥接，不能让读者在关键处断线
91	- Introduction 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
92	- Discussion 不应重复 Results 的具体数字——引用表格编号
93	- 同一解释不要在多处重复推导——主文写一次 + 指向 Appendix
94	- Limitations 段不要写成一个 17 行大段——拆分为 2-3 个子段按主题分组
95	
96	### 3.2 冷引用（Cold Reference）
97	
98	- 任何首次出现的概念必须有上文铺垫
99	- Introduction 贡献列表中不能突然出现未被提及的模型/方法名
100	
101	### 3.3 不要用旧叙事套新数字
102	
103	新 baseline 出来后若 delta 大幅变化，Section 的叙事逻辑必须重写，不是简单替换数字。
104	
105	### 3.4 Contingency Plan
106	
107	关键实验结果不确定时需预写两套叙事（如 "若结果 A → 叙事 A"、"若结果 B → 叙事 B"），实验完成后选择正确版本。提交前必须确认只留了一套。
108	
109	### 3.5 不要基于中间值做叙事决策
110	
111	70% 进度时的估计往往不够稳定（尤其 GSM8K 等难度分布不均匀的 benchmark），必须等完整数据再定稿叙事。
112	
113	---
114	
115	## 四、术语与一致性
116	
117	### 4.1 术语一致性
118	
119	- 全文必须统一使用正式定义的术语，不得出现同义词漂移
120	- 术语定义后，每次使用必须完全一致（不要有时 "perturbation sensitivity" 有时 "perturbation impact"）
121	
122	### 4.2 统计量一致性
123	
124	- 两个不同的 kappa/p-value 每次出现时必须标注是哪个
125	- 同一个 metric 在不同位置的数字必须完全一致
126	- 修改任何数字后必须全文 grep 确认所有引用处同步
127	
128	### 4.3 p-value 格式
129	
130	- 无前导零、3 位小数：`p = .003`（不是 `p = 0.003`）
131	- 当非劣效性 p<0.001 实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明
132	
133	### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致
134	
135	Taxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 "no new content"，示例中就不能包含新信息。
136	
137	---
138	
139	## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）
140	
141	### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）
142	
143	提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：
144	```
145	XX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \placeholder{
146	```
147	所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。
148	
149	### 5.2 编译检查
150	
151	- [ ] 编译零 error
152	- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用
153	- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox
154	- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）
155	- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）
156	- [ ] Review 版启用行号
157	- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢
158	
159	### 5.3 数据完整性
160	
161	- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过
162	- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认
163	- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明
164	- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差
165	- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明
166	
167	### 5.4 图表检查
168	
169	- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配
170	- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）
171	- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt
172	- [ ] 小数精度全表统一
173	- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线
174	- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含
175	- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）
176	
177	### 5.5 引用检查
178	
179	- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）
180	- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级
181	- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配
182	- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目
183	- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）
184	- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确
185	- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐
186	
187	### 5.6 匿名化检查
188	
189	- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）
190	- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径
191	- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）
192	- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）
193	- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露
194	
195	### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）
196	
197	- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围
198	- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充
199	- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）
200	- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用
201	- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间
202	- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours
203	- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整
204	- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况
205	- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）
206	
207	### 5.8 叙事完整性
208	
209	- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 "under greedy decoding"）
210	- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 "single seed" 另一个已是 "10 seeds"）
211	- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留
212	- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆
213	- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）
214	- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）
215	- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）
216	
217	### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查
218	
219	- [ ] **`grep "—" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）
220	- [ ] **`grep " – " paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）
221	- [ ] 无 "-ing," 分词短语结尾（如 "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."）
222	- [ ] 无连续 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"（同段最多 1 个）
223	- [ ] 无 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."
224	- [ ] 无 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "a myriad of" / "a plethora of"
225	- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段
226	
227	### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对
228	
229	- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）
230	- [ ] `\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段
231	- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐
232	
233	---
234	
235	## 六、Reviewer 审查标准
236	
237	### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）
238	
239	- 论文数字与实验数据不一致
240	- Abstract 与正文矛盾
241	- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）
242	- 未经验证的数据写入论文
243	- 占位符残留
244	
245	### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）
246	
247	- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）
248	- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑
249	- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 "3×3×3=27" 但实际只有 15 格）
250	- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain
251	- 旧版数据引用
252	
253	### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程
254	
255	1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics
256	2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）
257	3. 独立重算所有衍生值
258	4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL
259	5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内
260	
261	---
262	
263	## 七、常见反模式速查表
264	
265	| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |
266	|--------|------|----------|
267	| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |
268	| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |
269	| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |
270	| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |
271	| 叙事超越数据 | "confirmed" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |
272	| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |
273	| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |
274	| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |
275	| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |
276	| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |
277	| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |
278	| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |
279	| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |
280	| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |
281	
282	---
283	
284	## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills
285	
286	写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：
287	- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式
288	- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计
289	- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范
290	- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训
291	
292	---
293	
294	## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训
295	
296	1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改
297	2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备
298	3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理
299	4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证
300	5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿
301	
````

</details>

实际由正向效应驱动时，必须加括注说明\n\n### 4.4 例子与定义必须一致\n\nTaxonomy 中的定义和对应示例不能自相矛盾。如果定义说 \"no new content\"，示例中就不能包含新信息。\n\n---\n\n## 五、投稿前检查清单（Pre-Submission Checklist）\n\n### 5.1 占位符扫描（硬门禁）\n\n提交前必须 grep 扫描，以下模式要求**零匹配**：\n```\nXX.XX, PLACEHOLDER, [If , TODO, TBD, \\placeholder{\n```\n所有条件占位段落（`[If ...]`）必须替换为最终文本。\n\n### 5.2 编译检查\n\n- [ ] 编译零 error\n- [ ] 无 `??` 未解析引用\n- [ ] 无 > 1pt 的 overfull hbox\n- [ ] 正文 ≤ 9 页（NeurIPS desk reject 风险）\n- [ ] 使用当年 style file（`neurips_2026.sty`）\n- [ ] Review 版启用行号\n- [ ] 匿名审稿版无作者姓名、无致谢\n\n### 5.3 数据完整性\n\n- [ ] 论文所有数字与 registry/provenance 交叉验证通过\n- [ ] 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）独立重算确认\n- [ ] 所有 evaluation protocol 差异已在论文中说明\n- [ ] 多 seed 结果报告均值和标准差\n- [ ] 单 seed 数据在 Limitations 中声明\n\n### 5.4 图表检查\n\n- [ ] 所有 figure PDF 存在且路径匹配\n- [ ] 图表不超栏宽（单栏 5.5in / 全宽 6.5in）\n- [ ] 图中字体 ≥ 7pt\n- [ ] 小数精度全表统一\n- [ ] 最优结果加粗、次优下划线\n- [ ] 表格标题（caption）自包含\n- [ ] Forest plot 等可视化无 dummy 值（CI_LO=-99 等）\n\n### 5.5 引用检查\n\n- [ ] 引用覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**（任一缺失说明调研不充分）\n- [ ] 引用密度与目标会议近年录用论文同量级\n- [ ] 引用键与 bib 条目 1:1 匹配\n- [ ] 无孤儿 bib 条目\n- [ ] 无幻觉引用（所有被引论文实际存在）\n- [ ] 近年论文的年份/会议准确\n- [ ] 审稿人要求的引用全部补齐\n\n### 5.6 匿名化检查\n\n- [ ] 源代码无硬编码绝对路径（`/Users/...`, `/root/...`）\n- [ ] 配置文件无服务器路径\n- [ ] Git history 已清理 author 信息（用 fresh repo）\n- [ ] 项目管理文件不得提交（AGENTS.md, memory.md, registry.yaml, task_context/ 等）\n- [ ] API Keys 通过环境变量，无泄露\n\n### 5.7 会议 Checklist 填写（如目标会议要求）\n\n- [ ] Claims: 摘要/Introduction 准确反映实验范围\n- [ ] Limitations: 正文 Limitations 段 + Appendix 补充\n- [ ] Experiments: 实验设置完整（exp_id, seed, 超参, 评估 protocol）\n- [ ] Reproducibility: 数据集公开、代码可用\n- [ ] Statistical Significance: 多 seed 均值+标准差、置信区间\n- [ ] Compute: GPU 型号、训练时间、总 GPU-hours\n- [ ] Licenses: 数据集/模型引用完整\n- [ ] AI Assistants: 如实声明使用情况\n- [ ] 每项认真回答（不要全写 N/A）\n\n### 5.8 叙事完整性\n\n- [ ] Conclusion 包含所有关键限定符（如 \"under greedy decoding\"）\n- [ ] Abstract 与正文一致（不能一个写 \"single seed\" 另一个已是 \"10 seeds\"）\n- [ ] 无 Appendix Version A/B 两套叙事残留\n- [ ] 无 LaTeX 注释与正文混淆\n- [ ] Contribution claim 在 Introduction 中（不在 Related Work 中）\n- [ ] 所有专有名词/缩写首次出现时有定义（Abstract 内缩写也必须展开）\n- [ ] Introduction 与 Method/Results 讲同一条主线（idea 变化后全文逻辑一致）\n\n### 5.9 去 AI 风格检查\n\n- [ ] **`grep \"—\" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 em-dash 必须全删，最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n- [ ] **`grep \" – \" paper/*.tex` 全文清零**（解释性 en-dash 必须全删）\n- [ ] 无 \"-ing,\" 分词短语结尾（如 \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"）\n- [ ] 无连续 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"（同段最多 1 个）\n- [ ] 无 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"\n- [ ] 无 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\"\n- [ ] 自然段 5-8 句是正常的，不要每 2-3 句就换段\n\n### 5.10 模板与同类论文核对\n\n- [ ] LaTeX 模板从目标会议官网下载（不是他人转载、不是上一年）\n- [ ] `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项匹配 review / camera-ready 阶段\n- [ ] 写作前已参考 2-3 篇同领域近 1-2 年优秀论文，叙事节奏对齐\n\n---\n\n## 六、Reviewer 审查标准\n\n### 6.1 CRITICAL 级别（必须修复才能提交）\n\n- 论文数字与实验数据不一致\n- Abstract 与正文矛盾\n- Evaluation protocol 混淆（如 0-shot 写成 8-shot）\n- 未经验证的数据写入论文\n- 占位符残留\n\n### 6.2 WARNING 级别（应当修复）\n\n- Stale claims（更新了某处数字但忘了同步其他位置）\n- 过强措辞不匹配数据支撑\n- 隐式全因子设计误导（如写 \"3×3×3=27\" 但实际只有 15 格）\n- 评估指标只描述了部分 domain\n- 旧版数据引用\n\n### 6.3 数据一致性审查流程\n\n1. 从 registry 提取所有实验 metrics\n2. 对照论文中每一个内联数字（到行号级别）\n3. 独立重算所有衍生值\n4. 不一致处标注为 CRITICAL\n5. 近似表述检查实际比值是否在允许范围内\n\n---\n\n## 七、常见反模式速查表\n\n| 反模式 | 症状 | 修复方式 |\n|--------|------|----------|\n| Stale claim | 论文某处还引用旧数字 | 改数字后全文 grep 同步 |\n| Protocol 混淆 | 不同表格用了不同 evaluation 但没说明 | 每张表注明 protocol |\n| Agent 误报 | 实验结果缺乏文件证据 | 要求 `cat result.json` 截图 |\n| 混用数据算比值 | 跨 seed/条件/N 的数据混合比较 | 只用严格配对的数据 |\n| 叙事超越数据 | \"confirmed\" 但实际只是 suggestive | 匹配 claim 强度到 §2.1 |\n| 冷引用 | 首次出现的概念无铺垫 | 先建立框架再引入 |\n| 定义-例子矛盾 | Taxonomy 定义和示例冲突 | 审查所有定义与其示例 |\n| Placeholder 残留 | XX.XX, TODO, [If...] 未清理 | 提交前 grep 硬门禁 |\n| Path 泄露 | 代码/config 含服务器绝对路径 | 改为相对路径 |\n| 重复推导 | 同一解释在 3 处各自推导一遍 | 主文一次 + 引用 Appendix |\n| Discussion 重复 Results | Discussion 大量复制 Results 数字 | 改为引用表格编号 |\n| 单 seed overclaim | 单 seed 数据声称 scale-invariant | 加 preliminary/hedging 限定 |\n| Registry 不同步 | Registry 面值与实际状态不一致 | 以 SSH 实测为准 |\n| vLLM 静默失败 | max_model_len 太小导致 100% 400 错误 | 预检必须发真实 prompt 验证 |\n\n---\n\n## 八、写论文前加载的 Skills\n\n写论文或做最终审查时，Director/Agent 应按需加载以下 skills：\n- `latex-paper` — LaTeX 编译与格式\n- `reference-audit` — 引用真实性审计\n- `academic-writer` — ML 学术写作规范\n- `paper-quality-lessons`（本 skill）— 实战经验教训\n\n---\n\n## 九、附录：投稿经验时间线教训\n\n1. **论文数字锁定时机**：最终编译前 24h 锁定所有数字，之后只允许格式修改\n2. **GPU 不要空闲**：审稿期间如果 Agent 空闲，应该跑 ablation/扩展实验作为 rebuttal 储备\n3. **匿名化提前做**：不要等到最后一天才检查路径泄露，fresh repo 需要时间清理\n4. **9 页零余量策略**：NeurIPS 正文 9 页是硬限，每次新增内容必须等量压缩并编译验证\n5. **Contingency 叙事提前写**：关键实验完成前就写好两版叙事，避免最后一刻仓促改稿\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VKEmzR75pqks1JUX3zHDXk`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	As language models increasingly train on web text containing their own outputs, a recursive model$\to$data$\to$model loop arises, yet no method estimates how many such iterations a text has undergone.
3	We formalize \emph{recursive generation depth estimation} as ordinal classification and construct a 180K-passage benchmark spanning three model families, three decoding strategies, and four depths under controlled full-replacement chains at the 1--1.5B parameter scale.
4	A lightweight pipeline (19 statistical features classified by Random Forest) achieves d1--d3 balanced accuracy of 59--82\% across nine model--strategy cells, with $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} = 3$ in 8/9 cells.
5	Three findings emerge: (1)~depth signal concentrates in perplexity distribution tails, corroborated by classifier convergence and independent cross-perplexity features; (2)~decoding strategy affects cross-condition transfer more than model architecture, suggesting structurally distinct degradation trajectories; (3)~signal attenuates with scale (14B cross-reference accuracy ${\sim}44$\%, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$), with reference-model choice accounting for ${\sim}7$\,pp of the apparent degradation.
6	Perturbation-based and membership-inference baselines lack ordinal depth resolution, confirming that depth estimation requires distributional statistics beyond binary detection scores.
7	\end{abstract}
8	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:intro}
3	
4	The text on which large language models are trained increasingly contains their own outputs.
5	As synthetic content proliferates across the web, a recursive loop emerges: models trained on web corpora ingest text generated by earlier models, and their outputs feed into the next generation's training data \cite{shumailov2024_nature,alemohammad2024_mad}.
6	This model$\to$data$\to$model cycle is no longer hypothetical; it is already occurring in the wild. Yet whether the recursive generation depth of a text can be estimated from its statistical properties remains unstudied, a question that matters because first-generation synthetic text may be benign or even beneficial for training, while deeply recursive text drives the distributional collapse that degrades model quality \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.
7	In this work, we focus on the 1--1.5B parameter regime under \emph{controlled full-replacement chains}, where each generation fully replaces the prior training set, isolating the depth variable. Real-world contamination typically involves partial mixing (accumulation), where synthetic and human data co-exist at unknown proportions; the full-replacement setting provides an upper bound on depth signal strength, and we discuss implications for accumulation settings in \S\ref{sec:discussion}.
8	
9	Existing tools address adjacent but distinct problems.
10	Binary detectors \cite{binoculars2024,fast_detectgpt2024,diveye2026} collapse all depths $d \geq 1$ into a single category; watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} requires proactive embedding.
11	The model collapse literature \cite{shumailov2024_nature,dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} assumes depth is known a priori; \citet{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse} filter synthetic data via binary detection without distinguishing depths.
12	No existing method estimates the ordinal recursive generation depth of a text passage from its content alone.
13	
14	We find that recursive generation leaves quantifiable statistical fingerprints in text, and these fingerprints are \textbf{consistent in direction but scale-dependent in magnitude} across the three model families and three decoding strategies studied.
15	Specifically, the perplexity tail feature family (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{p75\_ppl}, \texttt{kurtosis\_ppl}) and surprisal variance emerge as dominant depth indicators, with group-level dominance robust across all 9 model--strategy configurations and three importance measures.
16	Following the tradition of benchmark-driven empirical studies in NLP, our contribution centers on task formalization and systematic empirical characterization rather than algorithmic novelty; the convergence of four classifiers within 0.8\,pp (\S\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) confirms that discriminative signal resides in feature design, not classifier architecture.
17	We formalize recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification and find that a lightweight pipeline suffices: compute 19 reference-model statistical features (effective dimensionality ${\approx}$4--5 due to multicollinearity; \S\ref{sec:atlas:features}) and classify with a Random Forest into $K{+}1$ depth classes.
18	To characterize how recursive generation reshapes these features, we construct the \emph{Depth Degradation Atlas}: a $3 \times 3$ grid of model families $\times$ decoding strategies spanning 4 depths (180K samples).
19	The atlas reveals strategy-dependent degradation trajectories and we introduce the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric to quantify how far into the recursion chain adjacent depths remain distinguishable (Figure~\ref{fig:problem}).
20	
21	\begin{figure*}[t]
22	\centering
23	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig_problem_illustration.pdf}
24	\caption{Recursive generation depth estimation. A seed corpus $\mathcal{D}_0$ feeds a LoRA finetuning--sampling loop producing successively deeper corpora. Given a passage~$x$, the task is to estimate its ordinal depth~$d$.}
25	\label{fig:problem}
26	\end{figure*}
27	Our contributions are:
28	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
29	    \item \textbf{Task formalization and metric.} We define recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification, introduce the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric, and construct a 180K-sample benchmark ($3$ model families $\times$ $3$ decoding strategies $\times$ $4$ depths).
30	
31	    \item \textbf{Depth Degradation Atlas.} The perplexity tail feature family dominates depth discrimination across all 9 cells; $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 8/9 cells (4 under nucleus depth extension), validated by 5-class extension (71--80\%); feature group ablation confirms perplexity tail dominance (76.6\% alone vs.\ 78.4\% full set).
32	
33	    \item \textbf{Systematic characterization.} Cross-model transfer 66.4\% (d1--d3: 55.4\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\%, preliminary 14B 44.1\% cross-reference ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$). We identify cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\%) as a boundary condition: decoding strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories, with strategy affecting transfer more than model architecture.
34	\end{itemize}
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model Collapse Theory.}
5	Recursive training on model outputs erodes distributional tails \cite{shumailov2024_nature}, an effect formalized as exponential tail decay via scaling laws \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails,seddik2024synthetic} and extended to autophagous loops in the MAD framework \cite{alemohammad2024_mad} and Markovian generation chains \cite{geng2026_markovian}.
6	Subsequent work refines the conditions under which collapse occurs: \citet{bertrand2024_stability} identify convergence to a fixed point under mixed real-synthetic retraining (providing theoretical grounding for our EDD ceiling, \S\ref{sec:method:theory}), \citet{shi2025_closer_look} reframe collapse as a generalization-to-memorization transition, and \citet{dohmatob2025_strong_collapse} extend guarantees to broader model classes.
7	Mitigation \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate,drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse,yi2026_escaping_collapse,falahati2026_curated,wenzek2020_ccnet}, data scaling under constraint \cite{muennighoff2023scaling}, non-monotonic dynamics \cite{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement}, convergence analysis \cite{marchi2024_heat_death,wang2026_contamination,knowledge_collapse2025,dohmatob2024_demystified}, and diversity decline \cite{guo2024curious,briesch2024selfconsuming,padmakumar2024diversity,sigma2026_spectral,synthetic_eggs2026} are active areas.
8	\citet{kazdan2025_collapse_or_thrive} systematically compare training workflows and show that data accumulation avoids collapse while pure replacement does not; \citet{suresh2025_rate_collapse} characterize the rate of collapse for fundamental distributions under recursive maximum-likelihood estimation.
9	\citet{schaeffer2025} argue that ``model collapse'' conflates multiple phenomena and call for operationally precise metrics; we respond by operationalizing tail-erosion predictions into a deployable ordinal classifier that reveals strategy-dependent trajectories and non-monotonic 7B behavior that the theory does not predict.
10	All assume depth is known a priori; we address the inverse problem of estimating depth from text alone.
11	
12	\paragraph{AI Text Detection.}
13	The detection literature \cite{wu2025_detection_survey} spans statistical methods (DetectGPT \cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt}, Fast-DetectGPT \cite{fast_detectgpt2024}, Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024}, DivEye \cite{diveye2026}, BiScope \cite{guo2024_biscope}, GLTR \cite{gehrmann2019_gltr}, Ghostbuster \cite{verma2024_ghostbuster}, DNA-GPT \cite{yang2024_dnagpt}), geometric approaches based on intrinsic dimensionality \cite{tulchinskii2023_intrinsic_dim}, and watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark}, with known limits from paraphrasing \cite{sadasivan2023_paraphrase}, distribution shift \cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}, and generator barriers \cite{pu2026_generator_barrier}.
14	\citet{wang2023_seqxgpt} address sentence-level attribution by using log-probability features from white-box LLMs.
15	M4-SemEval \cite{wang2024_m4_semeval}, MAGE \cite{li2024_mage}, and the PAN 2026 shared tasks \cite{bevendorff2026_pan} benchmark detection across domains, models, and adversarial conditions; RAID \cite{dugan2024_raid} targets binary detection across diverse attack vectors rather than ordinal depth estimation, making direct protocol adoption inapplicable to our task.
16	\citet{bevendorff2025_two_paradigms} distinguish authorship attribution (which model?) from verification (human or AI?); depth estimation introduces a third axis: which recursive iteration.
17	All these methods perform binary classification, discarding ordinal depth information; our approach outputs ordinal depth labels, complementing rather than replacing binary detection.
18	
19	\paragraph{Membership Inference and Data Provenance.}
20	Min-K\% Prob \cite{shi2024_minkprob} detects pretraining membership via low-probability token statistics, related to our perplexity features but limited to binary classification; Datamodels \cite{ilyas2022datamodels} trace predictions to influential training examples; cryptographically undetectable watermarks \cite{christ2024_undetectable} require generator cooperation.
21	TROVE \cite{zhu2025_trove} introduces fine-grained sentence-level provenance tracing with relationship classification, but targets source attribution rather than recursive depth.
22	Depth estimation occupies a distinct niche: ordinal, retroactive, and cooperation-free.
23	
24	\paragraph{Adjacent Tasks.}
25	EditLens \cite{editlens2026} estimates editing extent but does not model recursive chains; \citet{uchendu2020_attribution} classify \emph{which} model generated text; \citet{mohamed2025_broken_telephone} characterize \emph{how} text degrades under iterative generation without classifying which iteration a passage comes from.
26	Our task performs the inverse: ordinal depth estimation from text alone.
27	
28	\paragraph{Ordinal Classification.}
29	CORAL \cite{coral2020} enforces rank consistency via ordered binary tasks; CLOC \cite{cloc2025} extends contrastive learning with multi-margin ordinal losses; \citet{kasa2024_ordinality} benchmark ordinal techniques for NLP; \citet{janitza2016_ordinal_rf} show RF implicitly respects ordinal structure when inter-class distances are monotonic.
30	We adopt CORAL and CLOC as baselines (\S\ref{sec:generalization}).
31	
32	\paragraph{Decoding Strategy Effects.}
33	Decoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.
34	Our atlas (\S\ref{sec:atlas}) shows these strategies induce qualitatively different degradation trajectories under recursive generation.
```

CLOC \\cite{cloc2025} extends contrastive learning with multi-margin ordinal losses; \\citet{kasa2024_ordinality} benchmark ordinal techniques for NLP; \\citet{janitza2016_ordinal_rf} show RF implicitly respects ordinal structure when inter-class distances are monotonic.\nWe adopt CORAL and CLOC as baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}).\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding Strategy Effects.}\nDecoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \\cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.\nOur atlas (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas}) shows these strategies induce qualitatively different degradation trajectories under recursive generation.",

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Definition}
5	\label{sec:method:problem}
6	
7	Let $f_\theta$ denote a language model and $\mathcal{D}_0$ a corpus of human-written seed text.
8	We define a \emph{recursive generation chain} of depth $K$ as the sequence $\mathcal{D}_0, \mathcal{D}_1, \dots, \mathcal{D}_K$, where each $\mathcal{D}_k$ ($k \geq 1$) is produced by finetuning $f_\theta$ on $\mathcal{D}_{k-1}$ and sampling from the resulting model.
9	Depth $d{=}0$ denotes human-written text; depth $d{=}k$ ($k \geq 1$) denotes text generated by a model trained on depth-$(k{-}1)$ data.
10	
11	Given a text passage $x$, the task is to estimate its recursive generation depth $d \in \{0, 1, \dots, K\}$.
12	This is an ordinal classification problem: depths carry a natural ordering, and $d{=}1$ is structurally closer to $d{=}0$ than $d{=}3$ is, since each generation step compounds distributional drift.
13	
14	\subsection{Multi-Generational Benchmark Construction}
15	\label{sec:method:benchmark}
16	
17	We construct a benchmark spanning three axes: model family, decoding strategy, and generation depth.
18	
19	\paragraph{Model families.}
20	We select three 1--1.5B-parameter models with diverse pretraining corpora and architectures: Pythia-1.4B (EleutherAI), OLMo-1B (AI2), and GPT-2~XL (OpenAI, 1.5B).
21	Restricting to a narrow parameter range isolates the effect of architecture and training data from scale.
22	
23	\paragraph{Decoding strategies.}
24	Each model is paired with three decoding strategies: nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$) \cite{holtzman2020_nucleus}, temperature sampling ($\tau{=}0.9$), and top-$k$ sampling ($k{=}50$).
25	These span the dominant modes of open-ended generation and induce qualitatively different entropy profiles.
26	Our findings on feature importance and discriminability are limited to this grid; transfer to untested models, scales, or strategies is not established.
27	
28	\paragraph{Depth and scale.}
29	The primary benchmark covers depths d0--d3 across all $3 \times 3 = 9$ model--strategy cells, with 5{,}000 samples per depth per cell (180K samples total).
30	For depth extension experiments (\S\ref{sec:atlas}), we extend all three models under nucleus sampling to d0--d5.
31	
32	\paragraph{Generation pipeline.}
33	Starting from a Wikipedia subset ($\mathcal{D}_0$), each cycle: (1)~LoRA finetuning \cite{hu2022lora} on $\mathcal{D}_{k-1}$ ($r{=}16$, $\alpha{=}32$, lr $2{\times}10^{-4}$, effective batch 16, 3 epochs, dropout $0.05$; fresh adapter from the base model at each depth; full protocol in Appendix~\ref{app:lora_protocol}); (2)~sampling 5{,}000 passages (max 512 tokens, beginning-of-sequence (BOS) seeded) under the specified strategy; (3)~$\mathcal{D}_k$ feeds the next cycle.
34	Each depth is produced by a model trained on prior-depth data, not by sampling from the unmodified base model.
35	Beginning-of-sequence seeding may amplify depth signal relative to prompted generation; prompted-generation validation (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) confirms signal persistence albeit attenuated ($-$13.8\,pp).
36	No per-passage lineage exists across depths; cross-validation splits do not introduce leakage.
37	
38	\paragraph{Reference model.}
39	All statistical features (\S\ref{sec:method:features}) use Pythia-1.4B as fixed reference model.
40	This overlaps with one generator (Pythia), conferring a 4--11\,pp self-reference advantage on that pipeline (Appendix~\ref{app:ref_ablation}); Pythia results in Table~\ref{tab:main_matrix} are marked $^\dagger$ accordingly.
41	Alternative references yield 67--79\%, all exceeding $2.5{\times}$ chance (Limitations).
42	
43	\subsection{Statistical Feature Design}
44	\label{sec:method:features}
45	
46	We extract a feature vector $\mathbf{f}(x)$ comprising 19 candidate features from the reference-model perplexity distribution over each passage $x$; ablation (\S\ref{sec:atlas:features}) shows the top 5 achieve 97.6\% of full-set performance, and variance inflation analysis reveals VIF${>}$10 for 14--16 features; effective independent dimensionality is ${\approx}$4--5.
47	The features fall into four categories (Table~\ref{tab:features}).
48	
49	\begin{table}[t]
50	\centering
51	\small
52	\caption{The 19 statistical features comprising $\mathbf{f}(x)$, grouped by category. All perplexity and surprisal features are computed from the reference model (Pythia-1.4B).}
53	\label{tab:features}
54	\begin{tabular}{@{}llr@{}}
55	\toprule
56	Category & Features & $n$ \\
57	\midrule
58	Perplexity & mean, var, skewness, kurtosis, & 9 \\
59	statistics & p10, p25, p50, p75, p90 & \\
60	\addlinespace
61	Surprisal & mean, var, entropy & 3 \\
62	statistics & & \\
63	\addlinespace
64	Lexical & type--token ratio, hapax ratio, & 4 \\
65	diversity & bigram entropy, trigram entropy & \\
66	\addlinespace
67	Repetition & repeated 2/3/4-gram fractions & 3 \\
68	\bottomrule
69	\end{tabular}
70	\end{table}
71	
72	\emph{Perplexity statistics} (9 features) capture the per-token perplexity distribution via moments and quantiles (p10--p90); tail quantiles are especially informative as recursive finetuning erodes the high-perplexity tail (\S\ref{sec:atlas}).
73	\emph{Surprisal statistics} (3 features): mean, variance, and entropy of $-\log p(\text{token})$.
74	\emph{Lexical diversity} (4 features): type--token ratio, hapax ratio, bigram/trigram entropy.
75	\emph{Repetition} (3 features): repeated $n$-gram fractions for $n \in \{2, 3, 4\}$.
76	
77	\subsection{Effective Discriminable Depth}
78	\label{sec:method:edd}
79	
80	Aggregate multi-class accuracy obscures whether discriminability degrades at specific depth boundaries.
81	We propose the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) as an operational measure of how far into a recursive chain adjacent depths remain distinguishable.
82	
83	\paragraph{Pairwise discriminability.}
84	Let $\mathcal{D} = \{0, 1, \dots, K\}$ denote the depth set and $T \in (0.5, 1.0]$ a discrimination threshold.
85	For each adjacent pair $(d, d{+}1)$, we define the pairwise discriminability $\mathrm{AUC}(d, d{+}1)$ as the area under the ROC curve of a dedicated binary Random Forest classifier (500 trees, $\lfloor\sqrt{19}\rfloor$ candidate features per split) trained on class-balanced data from $\mathbf{f}(x)$ (\S\ref{sec:method:features}) to distinguish depth-$d$ from depth-$(d{+}1)$ samples, evaluated via 5-fold stratified cross-validation.
86	Each pair uses an independent classifier to avoid decision boundary dilution from multi-class settings.
87	
88	\begin{definition}[Effective Discriminable Depth]
89	\label{def:edd}
90	The \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} at threshold $T$ is:
91	\begin{multline}
92	\mathrm{EDD}_T = \max \bigl\{ d^*{+}1 \,:\, \\
93	\forall\, d' \leq d^*,\; \mathrm{AUC}(d', d'{+}1) \geq T \bigr\}
94	\label{eq:edd}
95	\end{multline}
96	where $d^* \in \{0, \dots, K{-}1\}$. If\, $\mathrm{AUC}(0,1) < T$, then $\mathrm{EDD}_T = 0$.
97	\end{definition}
98	
99	$\mathrm{EDD}_T$ reports the longest \emph{contiguous} discriminable prefix: the deepest $d$ such that every pair from $(0,1)$ through $(d{-}1, d)$ exceeds $T$.
100	All AUC profiles in our primary benchmark (9 cells $\times$ depths 0--3 plus 3 nucleus cells $\times$ depths 0--5 $=$ 12 profiles) decay monotonically; non-monotonic profiles (U-shaped recovery in Qwen2.5-7B, non-monotonic decay in Llama-3.1-8B base; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}) occur only in cross-scale extensions outside the primary atlas.
101	We adopt $T{=}0.70$ following the ``acceptable discrimination'' threshold in ROC analysis \cite{hosmer2000} (8/9 cells achieve $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} \geq 3$); sensitivity analysis (\S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}) shows $\mathrm{EDD}$ shifts by at most one level across $T \in \{0.65, 0.70, 0.80\}$ in all nine cells.
102	
103	\paragraph{Classifiers.}
104	Our primary classifier is a Random Forest (200 trees, unconstrained depth, 5-fold CV; EDD binary classifiers use 500 trees).\footnote{Statistical tests are not corrected for multiple comparisons; $p$-values should be interpreted as exploratory.}
105	The RF produces ordinally consistent predictions: 98.2\% of errors fall on adjacent depths ($|\Delta| \leq 1$), quadratic weighted kappa (QWK)\,=\,0.89.
106	Comparisons against other classifiers and detection baselines appear in \S\ref{sec:generalization}.
```

```text
1	\subsection{Theoretical Grounding}
2	\label{sec:method:theory}
3	
4	Recursive generation contracts the support of the learned distribution, with low-probability tokens lost first \cite{shumailov2024_nature}: tail mass $P_k(\mathrm{ppl} > t)$ decreases monotonically in depth $k$.
5	This tail erosion predicts the perplexity tail feature family as a depth marker, confirmed by its dominance across all 9 cells (\S\ref{sec:atlas:features}).
6	Surprisal variance captures complementary distributional narrowing: as the support contracts, per-token surprise becomes more predictable.
7	
8	\paragraph{EDD upper bound.}
9	Tail mass at depth $k$ scales as $\alpha^k$ for a contraction factor $\alpha \in (0,1)$ \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; EDD reaches its upper bound when adjacent-depth feature distance falls below classifier resolution.
10	Our empirical AUC-excess contraction (ratio 0.80 at d1v2$\to$d2v3, 0.69 at d3v4$\to$d4v5; \S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}) is consistent with geometric decay approaching a fixed point \cite{bertrand2024_stability}.
11	5-class extensions to d4 (71--80\%, chance = 20\%; Appendix~\ref{app:depth4_ext}) confirm depth~4 discriminability; mean perplexity saturates before tail statistics, placing a physical ceiling on EDD.
12	
13	\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}
14	Larger models approximate the training distribution more faithfully per step ($\alpha$ closer to 1), predicting weaker per-step signal.
15	Qwen2.5-14B supports this: d0-vs-d1 AUC drops to 0.643 (vs.\ ${>}0.99$ at 1--1.5B; \S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}); however, 7--8B results are mixed ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ ranging from 4 to 5), suggesting confounds (QLoRA quantization, architecture) interact with the scale effect (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
16	
17	\paragraph{Strategy-dependent convergence.}
18	The contraction rate $\alpha$ varies with decoding strategy.
19	Temperature scaling ($\tau < 1$) sharpens the logit distribution uniformly and accelerates convergence to the mode; temperature depth-$d$ text should therefore resemble nucleus depth-$(d{+}1)$ text in tail statistics.
20	GPT-2~XL confirms this: temperature d1 median \texttt{p90\_ppl} (92.2) matches the nucleus d2 median (92.3, IQR 75.8--112.5).
21	Top-$k$ truncation removes the low-probability tokens that carry depth signal, predicting weaker discriminability; top-$k$ cells yield the lowest accuracy (68.9--75.8\%) across the atlas (\S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}).
22	
```

```text
1	\section{The Depth Degradation Atlas}
2	\label{sec:atlas}
3	
4	We present the Depth Degradation Atlas: a systematic empirical map of how statistical fingerprints evolve across recursive generation depths, models, and decoding strategies.
5	The atlas spans $3 \times 3 = 9$ model--strategy cells at the 1--1.5B parameter scale, evaluated at depths $d \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$, with depth extensions to $d{=}5$ for cross-model EDD validation (\S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}).
6	
7	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
8	\subsection{In-Distribution Performance}
9	\label{sec:atlas:id}
10	
11	Table~\ref{tab:main_matrix} reports four-class Random Forest accuracy (5-fold CV, 200 trees) across all nine cells.
12	Accuracy ranges from 68.9\% (Pythia, top-$k$) to 85.9\% (GPT-2~XL, temperature) four-class (d1--d3 balanced: 58.9--81.5\%), all exceeding the 25\%/33\% chance baselines.
13	Two axis effects emerge.
14	GPT-2~XL achieves the highest accuracy under every strategy (75.8--85.9\%), followed by OLMo (74.4--82.3\%) and Pythia (68.9--78.7\%).
15	Top-$k$ sampling yields the lowest accuracy in every model.
16	The mean within-model strategy gap is 9.3~pp, comparable to the 7.2~pp mean within-strategy model gap; decoding strategy contributes at least as much as model family to performance variance.
17	
18	\begin{table}[t]
19	\centering
20	\small
21	\caption{Four-class RF accuracy (\%) with bootstrap 95\% CI (${\pm}$half-width; $B{=}1{,}000$). RF with 200 trees, evaluated via 5-fold stratified CV (seed=42). Parenthesized: d1--d3 balanced accuracy from the same 4-class RF (chance = 25\% / 33\%). Depth-0 recall exceeds 97\% in all cells. Within-distribution ordinal quality: mean absolute error (MAE)\,=\,0.23, QWK\,=\,0.89 (QWK$_{\text{d1--d3}}$\,=\,0.74); 98.2\% of errors fall on adjacent depths ($|\Delta| \leq 1$). $^\dagger$Self-reference scoring (Pythia-1.4B is both generator and reference model); 4--11\,pp advantage over cross-model references (Appendix~\ref{app:ref_ablation}). Cross-cell comparisons involving Pythia should be interpreted with this caveat.}
22	\label{tab:main_matrix}
23	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
24	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
25	\toprule
26	& Nucleus & Temp.\ $\tau{=}0.9$ & Top-$k$ \\
27	\midrule
28	Pythia-1.4B$^\dagger$  & 78.7{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(71.8) & 76.8{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(69.3) & 68.9{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(58.9) \\
29	OLMo-1B     & 78.9{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(72.7) & 82.3{\scriptsize$\pm$0.5}\,(76.6) & 74.4{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(66.2) \\
30	GPT-2~XL    & \textbf{84.3}{\scriptsize$\pm$0.5}\,(79.6) & \textbf{85.9}{\scriptsize$\pm$0.4}\,(81.5) & \textbf{75.8}{\scriptsize$\pm$0.6}\,(68.1) \\
31	\bottomrule
32	\end{tabular}}
33	\end{table}
34	
35	\paragraph{Per-class recall.}
36	Depth-0 recall exceeds 97\% in all 9 cells.
37	Among generated depths, d2 consistently yields the lowest recall (44--73\%), followed by d3 (61--80\%) and d1 (72--91\%).
38	The d2 trough reflects its position at the center of the depth gradient, where features overlap with both d1 and d3, consistent with d2-vs-d3 as the EDD bottleneck (\S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}).
39	Full per-class recall appears in Appendix~\ref{app:per_class}.
40	
41	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
42	\subsection{Feature Consistency Across the Grid}
43	\label{sec:atlas:features}
44	
45	Multicollinearity is substantial (VIF${>}$10 for 14--16 of 19 features); importance rankings below indicate feature \emph{family} contributions rather than individual effects; permutation importance (Appendix~\ref{app:permutation}) partially mitigates collinearity bias \cite{strobl2008_conditional,hooker2021_permutation}.
46	Despite the 17.1~pp accuracy spread across cells, the perplexity tail statistics family (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{p75\_ppl}, \texttt{kurtosis\_ppl}) and surprisal variance (\texttt{var\_surprisal}) dominate depth discrimination in every setting.
47	Within this family, \texttt{p90\_ppl} and \texttt{var\_surprisal} are consistently prominent, though individual rankings should be interpreted cautiously given VIF${>}$10; group-level ablation (\S\ref{sec:atlas:features}, below) provides the collinearity-robust confirmation.\footnote{The group-level dominance of perplexity tail statistics is robust across importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$, $p{<}0.005$).}
48	Table~\ref{tab:feature_importance} shows the full per-cell ranking.
49	
50	Cross-model feature-rank Spearman correlation averages $\bar{\rho} = 0.868$ (range $[0.619, 0.954]$): models agree on which features carry depth information even when they differ in absolute accuracy.
51	Among strategies, top-$k$ yields the highest cross-model rank correlation ($\rho = 0.811$): the deterministic vocabulary truncation imposes a more uniform feature landscape across architectures, at the cost of weaker overall signal (\S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}).
52	
53	Feature ablation confirms this concentration: the top-5 features achieve 97.6\% of full accuracy (76.5\% vs.\ 78.4\%); group-level ablation shows perplexity features alone reach 76.6\%, surprisal 73.2\%, lexical diversity ${\leq}$47\%, and repetition ${\leq}$40\%.\footnote{Best single feature (\texttt{p90\_ppl}) achieves 58.3\%, providing a more competitive single-feature baseline than mean perplexity (25.1\%).}
54	
55	The feature hierarchy shifts with depth.
56	At shallow boundaries (d1 vs.\ d2), \texttt{var\_surprisal} dominates importance; at deeper boundaries (d3 vs.\ d4, d4 vs.\ d5), tail perplexity statistics (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{kurtosis\_ppl}) take over.
57	This transition aligns with model-collapse dynamics~\cite{shumailov2024_nature}: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution (captured by its variance), while later steps erode the perplexity tail (captured by upper quantiles and kurtosis).
58

75	OLMo, nuc.         & \textbf{p90\_ppl\,(.18)}, \textbf{var\_surp\,(.15)}, p75\,(.11), m\_surp\,(.07), ttr\,(.06) \\
76	OLMo, $\tau$       & \textbf{p90\_ppl\,(.21)}, \textbf{var\_surp\,(.16)}, p75\,(.12), m\_surp\,(.09), ttr\,(.05) \\
77	OLMo, top-$k$      & \textbf{p90\_ppl\,(.20)}, \textbf{var\_surp\,(.17)}, p75\,(.10), m\_surp\,(.07), m\_ppl\,(.05) \\
78	\bottomrule
79	\end{tabular}}
80	\end{table}
81

83	\centering
84	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_atlas_heatmap.pdf}
85	\caption{Atlas feature importance heatmap for the top-7 features. Rows: features ranked by mean Gini importance; columns: 9 model--strategy cells. Stars mark the two universal top-2 features (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{var\_surprisal}). Full 19-feature breakdown in Appendix~\ref{app:feature_ablation}.}
86	\label{fig:atlas_heatmap}
87	\end{figure}
88	
89	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
90	\subsection{EDD Analysis}
91	\label{sec:atlas:edd}
92	\label{sec:atlas:monotonicity}
93	
94	Depth features exhibit strong monotonic trends (Spearman $\rho = -1.0$ for perplexity tail statistics; Figure~\ref{fig:feature_trajectory}); inter-depth distances are non-uniform (d0-vs-d1 AUC ${>}$0.99 in all 9 cells, d2-vs-d3: 0.69--0.89).
95	
96	We extend the benchmark to depth $d{=}5$ for all three models under nucleus sampling.
97	Each model's depth-3 LoRA checkpoint is used to finetune and generate depth-4 data, and the process repeats for depth~5; feature extraction uses the same reference model (Pythia-1.4B).
98	We train dedicated binary RF classifiers (500 trees) for each adjacent pair, as defined in Eq.~\ref{eq:edd}.
99	
100	\paragraph{AUC decay and cross-model convergence.}
101	For Pythia-1.4B (nucleus), AUC decays monotonically from $0.999$ (d0v1) to $0.683$ (d4v5); AUC-excess contraction ratios (0.80 at d1v2$\to$d2v3, 0.69 at d3v4$\to$d4v5) show convergence toward indistinguishability accelerates with depth (Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay}).
102	The d3-vs-d4 AUC clusters near $T{=}0.75$ for all three models (Pythia $0.784$, OLMo $0.765$, GPT-2~XL $0.750$), while d4-vs-d5 converges to a narrow band ($0.681$--$0.684$, range $0.003$), suggesting an architecture-independent depth ceiling within the 1--1.5B nucleus setting.
103	Bootstrap testing ($H_0{:}\, \mathrm{AUC} \leq 0.75$) yields $p = 0.001$ (Pythia), $0.091$ (OLMo), $0.505$ (GPT-2~XL).
104	
105	\paragraph{EDD threshold sensitivity.}
106	Across the 9-cell benchmark (depths 0--3), all cells achieve $\mathrm{EDD}{=}3$ at $T{=}0.65$; at $T{=}0.70$, 8/9 cells; at $T{=}0.75$, 7/9; at $T{=}0.80$, 5/9.
107	Top-$k$ configurations lose $\mathrm{EDD}{=}3$ first (Pythia top-$k$ at $T{=}0.70$, OLMo top-$k$ at $T{=}0.75$); d2-vs-d3 is the consistent bottleneck.
108	
109	\begin{figure}[t]
110	\centering
111	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_auc_decay.pdf}
112	\caption{AUC decay curves for Pythia-1.4B (nucleus, depths 0--5) under three pipeline configurations: binary pairwise RF, 4-class RF, and 6-class RF. Horizontal dashed lines mark $T \in \{0.65, 0.70, 0.75, 0.80\}$. All curves converge below 0.70 at d4-vs-d5. Shaded bands: bootstrap 95\% CIs.}
113	\label{fig:auc_decay}
114	\end{figure}
115	
116	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
117	\subsection{Strategy-Dependent Trajectories}
118	\label{sec:atlas:strategy}
119	
120	The three strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories.
121	Temperature ($\tau{=}0.9$) yields the highest accuracy in 2/3 models (GPT-2~XL 85.9\%, OLMo 82.3\%) because uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to an attractor~\cite{shumailov2024_nature}, amplifying the fingerprint but creating a boundary reversal risk under cross-strategy transfer (\S\ref{sec:generalization}).
122	Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) produces the lowest accuracy (68.9--75.8\%) because hard vocabulary truncation removes depth-discriminative low-probability tokens.
123	The strategy effect (9.3\,pp range) exceeds the model effect (7.2\,pp); deployment-time knowledge of the decoding strategy matters at least as much as the generator model.
124

Kendall's $W{=}0.90$, $p{<}0.005$).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:feature_importance} shows the full per-cell ranking.\n\nCross-model feature-rank Spearman correlation averages $\\bar{\\rho} = 0.868$ (range $[0.619, 0.954]$): models agree on which features carry depth information even when they differ in absolute accuracy.\nAmong strategies, top-$k$ yields the highest cross-model rank correlation ($\\rho = 0.811$): the deterministic vocabulary truncation imposes a more uniform feature landscape across architectures, at the cost of weaker overall signal (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}).\n\nFeature ablation confirms this concentration: the top-5 features achieve 97.6\\% of full accuracy (76.5\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%); group-level ablation shows perplexity features alone reach 76.6\\%, surprisal 73.2\\%, lexical diversity ${\\leq}$47\\%, and repetition ${\\leq}$40\\%.\\footnote{Best single feature (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}) achieves 58.3\\%, providing a more competitive single-feature baseline than mean perplexity (25.1\\%).}\n\nThe feature hierarchy shifts with depth.\nAt shallow boundaries (d1 vs.\\ d2), \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} dominates importance; at deeper boundaries (d3 vs.\\ d4, d4 vs.\\ d5), tail perplexity statistics (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}, \\texttt{kurtosis\\_ppl}) take over.\nThis transition aligns with model-collapse dynamics~\\cite{shumailov2024_nature}: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution (captured by its variance), while later steps erode the perplexity tail (captured by upper quantiles and kurtosis).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Top-5 features by Gini importance. \\textbf{Bold}: top-2. \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} top-2 in 9/9; \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} 8/9 under SHAP (7/9 under Gini). Individual rankings are approximate due to multicollinearity (VIF${>}$10 for 14--16/19 features).}\n\\label{tab:feature_importance}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l l@{}}\n\\toprule\nCell & Top-5 features (Gini importance, ranked) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia, nuc.       & \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.17)}, \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.15)}, m\\_ppl\\,(.11), var\\_ppl\\,(.08), p75\\,(.07) \\\\\nPythia, $\\tau$     & \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.17)}, \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.14)}, m\\_ppl\\,(.11), p75\\,(.07), m\\_surp\\,(.07) \\\\\nPythia, top-$k$    & \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.16)}, \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.12)}, m\\_ppl\\,(.11), var\\_ppl\\,(.07), m\\_surp\\,(.06) \\\\\nGPT-2\\,XL, nuc.    & \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.21)}, \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.21)}, p75\\,(.10), m\\_surp\\,(.07), m\\_ppl\\,(.06) \\\\\nGPT-2\\,XL, $\\tau$  & \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.25)}, \\textbf{p75\\,(.15)}, m\\_surp\\,(.13), var\\_surp\\,(.12), p50\\,(.05) \\\\\nGPT-2\\,XL, top-$k$ & \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.24)}, \\textbf{p75\\,(.12)}, var\\_surp\\,(.10), m\\_surp\\,(.09), ttr\\,(.04) \\\\\nOLMo, nuc.         & \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.18)}, \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.15)}, p75\\,(.11), m\\_surp\\,(.07), ttr\\,(.06) \\\\\nOLMo, $\\tau$       & \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.21)}, \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.16)}, p75\\,(.12), m\\_surp\\,(.09), ttr\\,(.05) \\\\\nOLMo, top-$k$      & \\textbf{p90\\_ppl\\,(.20)}, \\textbf{var\\_surp\\,(.17)}, p75\\,(.10), m\\_surp\\,(.07), m\\_ppl\\,(.05) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_atlas_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Atlas feature importance heatmap for the top-7 features. Rows: features ranked by mean Gini importance; columns: 9 model--strategy cells. Stars mark the two universal top-2 features (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}, \\texttt{var\\_surprisal}). Full 19-feature breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation}.}\n\\label{fig:atlas_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{EDD Analysis}\n\\label{sec:atlas:edd}\n\\label{sec:atlas:monotonicity}\n\nDepth features exhibit strong monotonic trends (Spearman $\\rho = -1.0$ for perplexity tail statistics; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature_trajectory}); inter-depth distances are non-uniform (d0-vs-d1 AUC ${>}$0.99 in all 9 cells, d2-vs-d3: 0.69--0.89).\n\nWe extend the benchmark to depth $d{=}5$ for all three models under nucleus sampling.\nEach model's depth-3 LoRA checkpoint is used to finetune and generate depth-4 data, and the process repeats for depth~5; feature extraction uses the same reference model (Pythia-1.4B).\nWe train dedicated binary RF classifiers (500 trees) for each adjacent pair, as defined in Eq.~\\ref{eq:edd}.\n\n\\paragraph{AUC decay and cross-model convergence.}\nFor Pythia-1.4B (nucleus), AUC decays monotonically from $0.999$ (d0v1) to $0.683$ (d4v5); AUC-excess contraction ratios (0.80 at d1v2$\\to$d2v3, 0.69 at d3v4$\\to$d4v5) show convergence toward indistinguishability accelerates with depth (Figure~\\ref{fig:auc_decay}).\nThe d3-vs-d4 AUC clusters near $T{=}0.75$ for all three models (Pythia $0.784$, OLMo $0.765$, GPT-2~XL $0.750$), while d4-vs-d5 converges to a narrow band ($0.681$--$0.684$, range $0.003$), suggesting an architecture-independent depth ceiling within the 1--1.5B nucleus setting.\nBootstrap testing ($H_0{:}\\, \\mathrm{AUC} \\leq 0.75$) yields $p = 0.001$ (Pythia), $0.091$ (OLMo), $0.505$ (GPT-2~XL).\n\n\\paragraph{EDD threshold sensitivity.}\nAcross the 9-cell benchmark (depths 0--3), all cells achieve $\\mathrm{EDD}{=}3$ at $T{=}0.65$; at $T{=}0.70$, 8/9 cells; at $T{=}0.75$, 7/9; at $T{=}0.80$, 5/9.\nTop-$k$ configurations lose $\\mathrm{EDD}{=}3$ first (Pythia top-$k$ at $T{=}0.70$, OLMo top-$k$ at $T{=}0.75$); d2-vs-d3 is the consistent bottleneck.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_auc_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{AUC decay curves for Pythia-1.4B (nucleus, depths 0--5) under three pipeline configurations: binary pairwise RF, 4-class RF, and 6-class RF. Horizontal dashed lines mark $T \\in \\{0.65, 0.70, 0.75, 0.80\\}$. All curves converge below 0.70 at d4-vs-d5. Shaded bands: bootstrap 95\\% CIs.}\n\\label{fig:auc_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Strategy-Dependent Trajectories}\n\\label{sec:atlas:strategy}\n\nThe three strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories.\nTemperature ($\\tau{=}0.9$) yields the highest accuracy in 2/3 models (GPT-2~XL 85.9\\%, OLMo 82.3\\%) because uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to an attractor~\\cite{shumailov2024_nature}, amplifying the fingerprint but creating a boundary reversal risk under cross-strategy transfer (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}).\nTop-$k$ ($k{=}50$) produces the lowest accuracy (68.9--75.8\\%) because hard vocabulary truncation removes depth-discriminative low-probability tokens.\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp range) exceeds the model effect (7.2\\,pp); deployment-time knowledge of the decoding strategy matters at least as much as the generator model.\n",

1	\section{Generalization and Robustness}
2	\label{sec:generalization}
3	
4	We test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).
5	
6	\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}
7	\label{sec:gen:baselines}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.
10	Four classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.
11	
12	\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}
13	DeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA) underperforms RF by 16--32\,pp (d2 recall 8.4\%); RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive; 64.5\%) narrows the gap but remains 14\,pp below RF.
14	
15	\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}
16	Perturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\% Prob, 33.2\%; Min-K\%++ \cite{zhang2024_minkpp} comparable) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}); CORAL calibration does not close the gap (d1--d3: 40.4\% vs.\ RF 71.6\%).
17	Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\ our 78.4\%); merging all 30 features yields 82.6\% (Appendix~\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}
20	Single-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.
21	
22	\begin{table}[t]
23	\centering
24	\small
25	\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\% / 33.3\% (d1--d3). $^\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}
26	\label{tab:baselines}

105	Cross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\% (four-class: 59.1\%), 7\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).
106	Decoding strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories; a two-stage pipeline recovers 11.5\,pp but presupposes known strategy (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
107	Our depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.
108	
109	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
110	\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}
111	\label{sec:gen:crossscale}
112	
113	At 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\% ($2{\times}$ chance, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\% ($-$6.8\,pp) but raises $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\sim}$7\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
114	Cross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.

Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d0 recall $\\geq$89\\% inflates headline; d1--d3 mean: 45.9\\%.}\n\\label{tab:cross_strategy}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Pythia & OLMo & GPT-2 XL \\\\\n & Acc (\\%) & Acc (\\%) & Acc (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nnucleus & \\textbf{69.4} & \\textbf{65.4} & \\textbf{59.9} \\\\\ntemp ($\\tau{=}0.9$) & 62.8 & 58.2 & 56.1 \\\\\ntop-$k$ ($k{=}50$) & 51.5 & 54.9 & 53.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nDecoding strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories; a two-stage pipeline recovers 11.5\\,pp but presupposes known strategy (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nCross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.",

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	Depth signal concentrates in perplexity tails because recursive training shifts the bulk distribution only gradually; erosion appears first in upper quantiles \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.
5	The strategy effect (9.3\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\,pp) implies auditing pipelines must account for decoding configuration.
6	Full-replacement chains provide the discriminability ceiling (d1--d3: 59--82\%); pilot accumulation ($-$14 to $-$15\,pp) and 14B cross-reference ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$: 0${\to}$2, ${\sim}$7\,pp reference-model mismatch) confirm signal survives but attenuates with mixing and scale.
7	
8	These findings motivate depth-aware curation (retaining shallow synthetic data while filtering deeply recursive passages; \citealp{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}) and highlight open directions including non-monotonic 7--8B AUC profiles (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
```

</details>

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Full-replacement vs.\ accumulation.}
5	All experiments use full-replacement recursive chains, where each depth fully replaces the prior training corpus.
6	Real-world contamination typically involves accumulation, where synthetic and human data co-exist at unknown proportions \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.
7	Pilot accumulation experiments show 14--15\,pp attenuation relative to full-replacement; our results therefore represent an upper bound on feature separability, and extending the framework to accumulation settings is a priority for future work.
8	
9	\paragraph{Scale and generalization boundaries.}
10	The primary benchmark uses 1--1.5B parameter models with controlled LoRA fine-tuning.
11	At 14B, cross-reference accuracy drops to 44.1\% with $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$ (nucleus/temperature); reference-model choice accounts for ${\sim}$7\,pp of the apparent degradation, and the remaining signal attenuation constitutes a scale-dependent boundary for the current feature set.
12	Cross-strategy transfer drops d1--d3 accuracy to 45.9\%, and on web-crawl data (C4) false positive rates reach 22\%; these gaps narrow with confidence thresholding and strategy-aware pipelines but are not fully resolved.
13	Additional caveats (reference model dependency, ${\leq}$11\,pp; single LoRA seed, ${\sim}$1--3\,pp uncertainty; adversarial robustness; passage length sensitivity) are detailed in the Appendix.
14	
15	\section*{Ethics Statement}
16	
17	This work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.
18	No human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.
19	Our method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.
20	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
21	AI assistants were used in an auxiliary role for coding support and language polishing.
22	All scientific decisions, experimental design, analysis, and core writing were conducted by the authors.
```

1	\section{LoRA Fine-Tuning Protocol}
2	\label{app:lora_protocol}
3	
4	Table~\ref{tab:lora_protocol} lists the full LoRA fine-tuning configuration used for recursive chain construction (\S\ref{sec:method:benchmark}).
5	At each depth $k$, a fresh LoRA adapter is initialized on the base model and trained on $\mathcal{D}_{k-1}$; adapters are not inherited across depths.
6	The HuggingFace Trainer default linear decay schedule is used with no warmup.
7	Target modules are model-specific: \texttt{query\_key\_value} (Pythia), \texttt{att\_proj} (OLMo), and \texttt{c\_attn} (GPT-2~XL).
8	Cross-scale experiments (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) use QLoRA (4-bit NF4) with $r{=}16$, $\alpha{=}32$.

35	\section{Additional Results}
36	\label{app:additional}
37	
38	\subsection{Top-$k$ Feature Trajectories}
39	\label{app:topk_trajectories}
40	
41	Figure~\ref{fig:feature_trajectory} shows feature trajectories for nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) and temperature ($\tau{=}0.9$) sampling.
42	Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) trajectories follow the same monotonically decreasing trend for all features but with compressed inter-depth spacing, consistent with the signal attenuation effect discussed in \S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}.
43	Concretely, the d0$\to$d1 drop in \texttt{p90\_ppl} under top-$k$ is 78\% (vs.\ 87\% for nucleus), and the d2$\to$d3 reduction is 31\% (vs.\ 39\% for nucleus).
44	Despite the magnitude reduction, feature rank ordering across depths is preserved, which explains why ordinal classifiers maintain directionality even under top-$k$ conditions.
45	
46	\subsection{Permutation Importance}
47	\label{app:permutation}
48	
49	To verify that the Gini-based importance rankings in \S\ref{sec:atlas:features} are not an artifact of the impurity measure, we compute permutation importance (5-fold CV, 10 repeats) for all 9 atlas cells.
50	The top-2 features are stable across measures: \texttt{p90\_ppl} ranks in the top 2 in 9/9 cells under both Gini and permutation importance, and \texttt{var\_surprisal} in 8/9 (vs.\ 7/9 for Gini, with the additional cell being OLMo top-$k$; the single SHAP exception is GPT-2~XL top-$k$, where \texttt{p75\_ppl} displaces \texttt{var\_surprisal} to rank 3).
51	Kendall's $W{=}0.90$ across all three methods (Gini, permutation, SHAP) indicates strong concordance ($p{<}0.005$).
52	Pairwise Spearman correlations are: SHAP--Gini $\rho{=}0.93$, SHAP--permutation $\rho{=}0.84$, Gini--permutation $\rho{=}0.76$.
53	Per-cell top-5 overlap between SHAP and permutation importance is 3--4 out of 5 features; the aggregated (mean-across-cells) top-5 sets are identical; the top features are therefore robust to the choice of importance measure at the aggregate level, though per-cell rankings exhibit model-dependent variation.
